# Syncari Repository Project Brief

Generated from local source inspection of the extracted workspace at `c:\Users\laksh\Downloads\Syncari`.
This brief explains what the codebase is, what each major area does, how the frontend and backend fit together, and what functionality is present.
The workspace is not a single Git checkout; it is a collection of source snapshots and research files.

> Scope note: this document is based on direct inspection of the local files, Maven metadata, npm metadata, README files, route constants, controllers, store modules, docs, and source-tree inventories.


## 1. Executive Summary

Syncari is represented here as a data automation and data operations platform made of a Java/Spring backend monorepo and a React/TypeScript frontend application.
The product vocabulary revolves around synapses/connectors, entity schemas, sync pipelines, field pipelines, data quality rules, data studio record views, insights dashboards, logs, notifications, access control, quick-start templates, and custom integrations.
The backend owns domain modeling, persistence, connector execution, API endpoints, sync processing, validation, security, data quality, analytics, and operational workers.
The frontend, named Spectrum, owns the browser UI used by operators and administrators to configure connectors, model schemas, build and test pipelines, inspect records, author insights, view logs, manage settings, and handle data quality workflows.
Two backend snapshots exist: `backend-master` and `backend-SYN-20253`.
Two frontend snapshots exist: `spectrum-master` and `spectrum-SYN-20252`.
The SYN snapshots are close to master but contain branch-specific additions around SyncAI, including backend SyncAI services and frontend SyncAI panel/chat components.
Additional business/research artifacts exist at the root as Word, Excel, and PDF files, but the runnable code is concentrated in the backend and spectrum directories.

## 3. Main Architectural Shape

- Backend runtime stack: Java 11, Maven, Spring Boot 2.1.5, Spring MVC controllers, Spring Data MongoDB repositories, Redis/Jedis usage, BigQuery analytics support, Kafka/pubsub style processing, and custom connector abstractions.
- Frontend runtime stack: React 18, TypeScript, Redux/Redux Toolkit, RTK Query style API slices, Reach Router, Ant Design 3, AG Grid, Highcharts, VisX, React Flow, Slate editors, Storybook, Jest, Cypress, and a Node proxy.
- Persistence model: MongoDB-oriented repositories are visible throughout `core`, with separate syncari/global repositories and customer-scoped repositories.
- API model: backend exposes REST endpoints from `arcade` and `karibu`; frontend store modules wrap these APIs by domain.
- Worker/processing model: `viper` appears to execute sync streams and pipeline stages; `connector` and `core` provide connector behavior; `visions` handles analytics queries.
- UI model: Spectrum is organized by product areas under `src/pages`, reusable controls under `src/components`, state/data access under `src/store`, and shared helpers under `src/utils`.
- Deployment model: backend has Maven modules plus Jenkins CI; frontend builds static assets, packages them with a Node proxy, and can run in a Docker container.

## 4. Backend Monorepo Overview

The backend root README says it is a monorepo for all backend projects and gives local run commands for `arcade` and `viper`.
The parent Maven POM packages modules under group `com.syncari`, Java version `11`, revision `DEV-SNAPSHOT`, and Spring Boot parent `2.1.5.RELEASE`.
Backend modules listed in the parent POM are `utils`, `connector`, `core`, `restutils`, `dbm`, `visions`, `viper`, `arcade`, and `karibu`.
A `microbenchmarks` module is present in the tree but not listed in the parent modules section inspected from `backend-master/pom.xml`.

Local backend run examples from the README:
- `mvn clean install -DskipTests -pl arcade spring-boot:run`
- `mvn clean install -DskipTests -pl viper spring-boot:run -Dspring-boot.run.arguments=--server.port=8083`

### Backend Module Responsibilities

- `arcade`: Main REST API surface for Spectrum and browser-facing operations. Contains many controllers for connectors, schemas, pipelines, data studio, dashboards, insights, notifications, auth, SSO, tokens, and support.
- `karibu`: API service layer with v1/v2 endpoints for users, synapses, pipelines, marketplace, quick start, transactions, reference data, PLG subscriptions, and realtime pipeline hooks.
- `core`: Primary domain library: models, repositories, services, validation, security, sync logic, mapper logic, data quality, actions, credentials, pipelines, schemas, webhooks, and shared business rules.
- `connector`: Connector framework and connector client support. Handles source/sink behavior and third-party system integration primitives.
- `restutils`: REST DTOs, transformers, and shared web/API utilities used by controller modules.
- `viper`: Spring Boot runtime for sync stream execution and pipeline processing. Tests reference save-to-sink, field pipeline execution, merges, deletes, loops, and transformation stages.
- `visions`: Analytics/query module. Contains BigQuery engine, analytics service, query cache, report request/response data objects, and metrics models.
- `dbm`: Database migration/management module. Has its own Spring Boot application and README.
- `utils`: Low-level shared utilities and common dependencies such as logging, collections, math, JSON, helper code, and common primitives.
- `smokescreen`: Go-based side component with Docker/Kubernetes manifests and ACL/config files, likely used as an outbound proxy or request guard.
- `microbenchmarks`: JMH benchmarks for focused performance measurement around expressions/filter processing.

### Backend Repository Model

Repositories are mostly Spring Data Mongo repositories. They are separated into syncari/global repositories and customer-scoped repositories.
Important repository concepts include `SyncariRepo`, `DraftableRepo`, monitorable repos, custom repository extensions, and MongoRepository specializations.

## 5. Frontend Spectrum Overview

Spectrum is the React/TypeScript frontend application.
Its README describes setup through `.env`, `npm install`, `npm run start`, optional proxy usage, mock server usage, Cypress testing, containerized execution, and LESS variable conversion.
The package metadata shows `name: spectrum`, `version: 0.98.0`, private package, and proxy target `http://localhost:8088`.
The Node engine requested by package metadata is Node `v20.18.3` and npm `10.8.2`.
The application uses Reach Router for routing, Redux/Redux Toolkit for state, Jest for unit tests, Cypress for end-to-end tests, Storybook for component development, and a Node proxy for backend routing.
Product code follows a convention where `src/pages` mirrors top-level UI/product areas, `src/store` mirrors API/state domains, and `src/components` holds reusable UI primitives.

## 6. Product Functionality Map

- **Authentication and identity**: Login, forgot password, reset password, password reset success, SSO routes, SAML IDP backend endpoints, profile pages, tokens, JWT filters, and authorization controllers.
- **Synapses and connectors**: Connector setup, connector metadata, credentials, connector activation/deactivation, custom synapses, SDK synapses, HTTP synapses, webhook synapses, connection tests, schema refreshes, and connector logs.
- **Schema Studio**: Schema browsing and editing around synapses/connectors, entities, versions, dynamic metadata, entity nodes, attribute nodes, and schema studio controllers.
- **Sync Studio**: Core area for modeling entities, action studio, entity pipelines, field pipelines, graph versions, pipeline validation, pipeline errors, documentation versions, and quick-start installation.
- **Pipeline graph editing**: React Flow/editor based UI plus backend graph models, mapping graph repositories, pipeline services, node validation, draft/published versions, tests, publish flows, and simulation support.
- **Field pipeline logic**: Field-level transformations, functions, conditions, lookups, merges, loops, references, deletion handling, numerical/date/text/list functions, and execution tests in `viper`.
- **Data Studio**: Entity record grids, reference data grids, record details, lineage/transactions, filtering, batch operations, data score display, and record action cells.
- **Data Quality Studio**: Dashboards, entity breakdown widgets, DFI rules, data quality categories, impact/status, rule configuration, and data score reporting.
- **Insights Studio**: Dashboards, datasets, datacards, chart configuration, authoring sidebar, ThoughtSpot/embed style areas, AI assisted datacard creation, sharing, and public/shared dashboard flows.
- **Logs and transactions**: Transaction logs, sync error logs, entity logs, pipeline logs, KPI endpoints, error catalogs, transaction details, and downloadable connector logs.
- **Reference data**: Reference data datasets, uploads, item CRUD, reference-data grids, sidebar sections, upsert modals/panels, and APIs in both backend and frontend store.
- **Imported files**: File/folder management, upload side drawer, previews, imported files API/store, file data controller, and file/folder metadata models.
- **Notifications**: Notification settings, error notification config, invitations, user info, settings routes, notification controllers, and v1/v2 API slices.
- **Access control**: ABAC and RBAC settings, role/privilege resources, ABAC policies/attributes/values, authorization controllers, and access-control store modules.
- **Quick Start**: Reusable templates or guided setup flows for sync studio, install/run/cancel/status APIs, quick-start authoring, library, install review, schema matcher, issue resolution, and post-installation views.
- **Custom actions**: Custom action definitions, action studio UI, share panel, action controller/core actions, and connector-side action behavior.
- **Dashboards and visualizations**: Legacy/new dashboards, datacards, data cards with datasets, chart viz data, Highcharts/VisX usage, dashboard layout, and dashboard sharing details.
- **Branding and organization**: Brand controller, branding store, organization reducer/thunks, subscriptions, PLG trial endpoints, instance feature flags, plans, quotas, and organization models.
- **Testing and validation**: Pipeline tests, simulation runs, validation store, graph validation utilities, validators, Jest tests, Cypress tests, Spring tests, embedded Mongo/Redis test config, and JMH microbenchmarks.
- **SyncAI branch feature**: SYN snapshots introduce SyncAI backend services, conversation/session models, rate limiting, sanitization, DTOs, prompt seeding, and frontend SyncAI chat/panel components for data-quality pipeline assistance.

## 7. Branch Snapshot Differences

The branch-like directories are not Git repositories locally, so differences were detected by comparing filenames between snapshot folders.

## 8. Build, Run, Test, And Tooling

- Backend build tool: Maven.
- Backend parent: Spring Boot starter parent 2.1.5.RELEASE.
- Backend Java version: 11.
- Backend local run: use Maven module selection for `arcade` or `viper`.
- Frontend build tool: npm scripts with custom webpack/react-scripts style scripts in `scripts/`.
- Frontend local start: `npm run start` after `.env` setup and `npm install`.
- Frontend proxy: `proxy/` Node server; can target local backend, integration backend, or mock server.
- Frontend mock server: `mock-server/`, started directly or via `npm run start-mock`.
- Frontend unit tests: Jest via `npm run test`, `npm run test-ci`, and coverage scripts.
- Frontend E2E tests: Cypress via `npm run cypress:localhost:open` or headless scripts.
- Frontend Storybook: `npm run storybook` or `npm run build-storybook`.
- Frontend container: `npm run build-all && npm run run-container`.
- Formatting/linting: ESLint, Prettier, JSON sorting, LESS-to-TS conversion, Husky prepare hook.
- Backend tests: Maven/Spring tests under each module, with embedded Mongo configuration visible in tests.
- Backend performance tests: JMH microbenchmark module.

## 13. Practical Mental Model For New Developers

Start with the product domain: Syncari connects external systems, models their data as entities/attributes, maps those models into pipelines, executes syncs, and provides operator tools for visibility and correction.
On the backend, `core` is the domain anchor. If a concept has persistence, validation, or business rules, look in `core` first.
If the concept is exposed to Spectrum as a browser-facing REST API, look in `arcade` controllers and corresponding DTO/transformer code in `restutils`.
If the concept is part of public/v1 integration APIs or operational API flows, inspect `karibu`.
If the concept runs data through pipeline stages, inspect `viper` and its stream/stage tests.
If the concept integrates with third-party SaaS systems, inspect `connector` plus the relevant connector models/services in `core`.
If the concept is analytics/reporting oriented, inspect `visions` and the insights/dashboard controller surface.
On the frontend, start with `src/utils/RouteConstants.ts` to identify the route, then move to `src/pages/<area>` for screen behavior.
For data fetching and mutations, inspect `src/store/<domain>/api.ts`, `thunks.ts`, reducers/slices, and types.
For shared UI, inspect `src/components`, especially graph, skull, validator, toolbar, list, modal, and navigation components.
For generated/configured forms or wizards, inspect the Skull config docs and components around `src/components/skull` and `src/store/skull`.
For tests, frontend unit tests live near source files or `__tests__`, Cypress tests live under `cypress`, and backend tests mirror module packages under `src/test/java`.

## 14. Risks And Caveats

- The local folders are source snapshots without Git metadata, so branch history, commit intent, and exact merge base are unavailable.
- The project is large; this brief focuses on architecture and functionality discovered from names, metadata, docs, routes, controllers, stores, and inventories.
- Some domain names have dual product/code vocabulary: the docs explicitly state `Synapse == Connector`, where Synapse is presentation vocabulary and Connector is code vocabulary.
- Both old and new versions of certain areas exist, such as data studio/data-studio-new, dashboards/new-dashboard, data-quality/data-quality-v2, and error-notifications/error-notifications-v2.
- The SYN snapshots add SyncAI files, but a semantic source diff of file contents was not performed here because no Git metadata is available.
- Running the full project likely requires external services: MongoDB, Redis, backend service ports, credentials, and/or integration targets.

## 15. Conclusion

This repository represents a mature enterprise data operations platform with separate backend and frontend monorepos.
The backend provides domain models, persistence, APIs, pipeline execution, connector abstractions, analytics, security, and service orchestration.
The frontend provides a broad operator/admin UI for configuring integrations, modeling schemas, building sync pipelines, managing data quality, browsing data, creating insights, and reviewing operational logs.
The branch snapshots show active work around SyncAI, adding AI-assisted data quality or rule/pipeline support on both backend and frontend.
A new contributor should orient around the route/controller/store triad: Spectrum route -> Spectrum store API -> backend controller -> core service/repository/model.
